**Jang (2002) — Two genetic factors within A:**

Factor 1 ("Compassionate concern"): Canada dominated by A1 (.67); Germany by A3 (.50), A4 (.42), A1 (.41). Factor 2 ("Social propriety"): Canada by A2 (.57), A5 (.40); Germany by A5 (.70), A2 (.34). A6: 56–58% facet-specific genetic variance in both samples. A1: 62% facet-specific genetic variance in Germany.

**Proposed genetic clustering:**

**Cluster G1 — Genetic A-factor core: {A2 Straightforwardness, A4 Compliance}**

Highest and most consistent genetic A-factor loadings across countries. Define the genetic A factor.

**Cluster G2 — Genetically semi-independent: {A1 Trust, A6 Tender-Mindedness}**

Both have >50% facet-specific genetic variance (Jang 2002). A1 cross-loads on N; A6 is genetically independent of both within-A factors. Weakly embedded in A.

**Genetically diffuse: A3 Altruism**

Bridges A, C, and E genetically. C cross-loading = .34–.36 across three countries — one of the most robust cross-domain genetic signals in the NEO system.

**Culturally variable: A5 Modesty**

Clean Politeness marker genetically in Western samples. Drops to .23 genetic A loading in Japan.

**Step 3: Convergence Assessment**

**Proposed within-domain groupings:**

**1. {A2 Straightforwardness, A4 Compliance} — the behavioral-compliance core.**

Three-stream convergence. Both increase developmentally. Both are structural Politeness markers. Both define the genetic A-factor core. Tightest unit in the domain.

*Caveat:* A4 loads genetically on the Compassion-like subfactor in Jang (2002, L, German data). The five-factor genetic model (Yamagata 2006, XL) puts A4 cleanly on A. Weight the XL finding higher.

**2. {A3 Altruism, A6 Tender-Mindedness} — phenotypic Compassion, genetically weak.**

Structural Compassion grouping (Soto & John 2017, XL). No developmental convergence. Genetically they don't cluster — A3 bridges to C/E, A6 is mostly facet-specific.

**3. A1 Trust — domain marker / N-boundary facet.**

Structurally orphaned. Genetically split between A and low-N. Only A facet with genetic influence on developmental change.

**4. A5 Modesty — culturally variable Politeness marker.**

Genetically clean Politeness marker in Western samples but drops out of A in Japan. Developmentally inert. Excluded from BFI-2.

**Self-check on pattern-matching:** I notice my groupings resemble published arguments that Big Five A is a phenotypic compromise that doesn't carve nature at genetic joints. Evidence independently supporting this: (a) A1's genetic N cross-loading replicates across 3 countries (Yamagata, XL); (b) A3's genetic C cross-loading replicates across 3 countries (Yamagata, XL); (c) A6's genetic facet-specificity replicates across 2 countries (Jang 2002, L). Evidence against: (a) genetic A factor has congruence ≥ .86 with phenotypic target (Yamagata); (b) genetic subfactors within A partially match Compassion/Politeness (Jang 2002).

**Step 4: Misbehaving Facets**

**A1 Trust** — the clearest misfit. Structurally orphaned (Soto & John 2017, XL). Genetically more N-like than A-like in Canada (Yamagata 2006, XL). 62% facet-specific genetic variance (Jang 2002, L). Only A facet with genetic influence on change (Bleidorn 2009). Behaves like a cognitive-evaluative construct (beliefs about others' intentions) correlating with A and low-N phenotypically.

**A3 Altruism** — genetically diffuse. Consistent C cross-loading .34–.36 across three countries (Yamagata 2006, XL). Bridges A, C, and E genetically. Flag for Conversation 6.

**A6 Tender-Mindedness** — genetically semi-independent. 56–58% facet-specific genetic variance (Jang 2002, L). HEXACO places equivalent content in Emotionality (Lewis & Bates 2014, M). Phenotypically clean Compassion marker, genetically autonomous.

**Cross-domain signals:**- A1 Trust → Neuroticism (inverted). Genetic N loading: −.59/−.39/−.29 (Yamagata 2006, XL). BFI-2 Trust: −.32 secondary on N (Soto & John 2017, XL). Strongest cross-domain affinity in the A domain.**

  - A3 Altruism → Conscientiousness. Genetic C loading: .35/.34/.36 (Yamagata 2006, XL). Most replicated cross-domain genetic signal.

  - N2 Angry Hostility → low Agreeableness. Genetic A loading: −.32/−.41/−.60 (Yamagata 2006, XL). In Japan, genetic A loading (−.60) ≈ genetic N loading (.56). N2 genetically bridges N and low A.

  - Roberts' Reliability facet straddles C/A.** Correlates almost equally with C and A (Jackson 2009, M). Content overlaps NEO C3 Dutifulness + A4 Compliance.**Step 5: Overall Domain Coherence**

**Agreeableness is held together phenotypically but fractures genetically.**

The phenotypic picture is moderately coherent: the BFI-2 three-facet model works, the BFAS two-aspect model works structurally, and the genetic A factor has reasonable congruence with the phenotypic target (.86–.97; Yamagata 2006, XL).

But beneath this:

  - **Genetic subfactors only partially match published aspects.** A4 Compliance loads genetically with the Compassion-like factor, not Politeness (Jang 2002, L). A6 Tender-Mindedness is mostly genetically independent. A1 Trust is mostly genetically independent. Two of six facets have >50% facet-specific genetic variance.**- Multiple A facets have substantial cross-domain genetic affinity.** A1 → N (3 countries). A3 → C (3 countries). N2 → low A (3 countries). The A domain's genetic boundaries are permeable.**- Developmental change is etiologically incoherent.** Every changing A facet has a different etiology (Bleidorn 2009). This is the most etiologically heterogeneous domain.**- Cross-cultural genetic stability of A is the weakest.** Canada–Japan congruence = .86 (Yamagata 2006, XL). A5 drops to .23 in Japan. A4 drops to .43. The genetic architecture of A is more culturally variable than any other Big Five domain.**- Compassion–Politeness distinction is real but weakly correlated.** r = .34 (Haehner 2025, L). Lower than all other within-domain aspect pairs except Openness–Intellect (.29).**What holds together:** {A2, A4} is the tight core — three-stream convergence. The phenotypic Compassion/Politeness distinction is structurally real. But the six NEO facets are not a cohesive genetic unit.

**Step 6: Gaps**

  - **Full pairwise genetic correlations between A facets.** Jang (2002) reports factor loadings, not the full 6×6 rG matrix. Cannot directly test whether aspect-mates share more genetic variance than non-aspect-mates.**- Cross-domain multivariate genetic analysis.** Both Jang (2002) and Lewis & Bates (2014) modeled facets within-domain only. Cross-domain signals (A1→N, A3→C) come from five-factor loadings (Yamagata 2006), not pairwise rG.**- Well-powered developmental trajectory data.** Bleidorn (2009) is M quality with 61 DZ pairs. Biometric decomposition of change is S quality.**- Item-level structural data for NEO-PI-R A items.** No study reports item-level factor structure for the full NEO A items. BFI-2 item data exists but covers only 4 items per facet.**- HEXACO-to-NEO genetic correlations.** Lewis & Bates (2014) gives HEXACO A genetics; other studies give NEO A genetics. No study links the two directly.**- Non-Western, non-Japanese genetic data.** Yamagata includes Japan but the sample is much younger. No African, Latin American, or South/Southeast Asian data.**- Observer-report developmental data for A.** All developmental data is self-report.**- A5 Modesty in modern structural systems.** Excluded from BFI-2 with no clear successor. Its structural home in three-facet-per-domain models is undefined.**

*Openness*********

**Openness to Experience Domain Profile: O1–O6**

**Step 1: Facet Profile Table**

**O1 Fantasy** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******Jang 1996 (M): h²=.34 (additive, AE). Jang 1998 (L): raw AE, residual h²=.25 (.42 of reliable) — **lowest residual h² in O domain**. Pilia 2006 (XL): no age or sex heterogeneity for O facets generally.

        **Developmental trajectory******

        Declines in adulthood. Schwaba 2022 (M): decline. Brandt 2023 (L): decreased. Seifert 2023 (XL): imagination items ("has a vivid fantasy/imagination") declined across all three independent panels — one of the most replicated item-level findings in the paper. Brandes 2020 (L): ICID-S Openness facet (blends O1/O3/O4 content) declined ages 9–13. Ringwald 2024 (L): BFI-2 Creative Imagination (partial O1 correspondent) decreased ages 14–23.

        **Structural placement******DeYoung 2007: Openness aspect (.64) with secondary Intellect loading (.45) — **strongest dual-loading of any O facet**. Soto & John 2017: Creative Imagination is the BFI-2 third facet, but modest convergence with NEO Fantasy.

        **Genetic correlations******Nikolašević 2021 (S): rG=.23 with fluid intelligence (APM) — significant, survives FDR. This places O1 with the Intellect facets (O5 rG=.38, O6 rG=.18), **not with its Openness aspect-mates** (O2 rG=−.05, O3 rG=.03).

        **Cross-taxonomy******

        Not specifically mapped in Ludeke 2019 (aspect-level only). Ashton & Lee 2016: HEXACO Creativity is approximately the nearest correspondent — flat developmentally.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Genetic-structural mismatch.** Structurally loads on Openness aspect, but intelligence genetic correlations align with Intellect facets. Nikolašević 2021 (S) calls this "novel and not in line with previous findings." Also has lowest residual heritability in the domain (Jang 1998, L), suggesting less genetically distinct specificity than other O facets.

**O2 Aesthetics** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******Jang 1996 (M): d²=.57 (nonadditive, ADE model) — **highest heritability of any O facet** and among the highest of any NEO facet. Jang 1998 (L): residual h²=.37 (.51 of reliable) — **highest residual h² in O domain**. Pilia 2006 (XL): no age heterogeneity. Hansen 2025 (M): BFI-2 Aesthetic Sensitivity h²=.58, rA=.69 with musical sensibility.

        **Developmental trajectory******Predominantly **stable** in adulthood, with some study-dependent variation. Terracciano 2005 (L): essentially flat across lifespan. Schwaba 2022 (M): flat. Brandt 2023 (L): decreased (but declining rank-order stability in old age per permutation test). Soto et al. 2011 (M): gender-differentiated pattern — rises for males in youth, rises for females in midlife. Ashton & Lee 2016 (M): HEXACO Aesthetic Appreciation rises ~0.3 SD from mid-20s to 70. Seifert 2023 (XL): "values artistic, aesthetic experiences" increased with age (SOEP panel). **Net assessment**: stable through most of adulthood; may increase in some populations; mild decline possible in late old age.

        **Structural placement******DeYoung 2007: clean Openness aspect marker. Hansen 2025 (M): β=.44 with musical sensibility — **strongest predictor**, carrying essentially the entire domain-level association. O1 Intellectual Curiosity drops to β=.07 when O2 is in the model.

        **Genetic correlations******Nikolašević 2021 (S): rG=−.05 with APM — **zero genetic correlation with fluid intelligence**. This cleanly differentiates O2 from O5 Ideas (rG=.38).

        **Cross-taxonomy******

        Ludeke 2019 (L): Openness aspect dominates HEXACO O (β=.63). Ashton & Lee 2016: HEXACO Aesthetic Appreciation is the nearest correspondent.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Developmental similarity with O5 Ideas** despite being in opposite aspects. Both are stable/resilient across adulthood while other O facets decline. This is a cross-aspect convergence that doesn't fit the published aspect structure.

**O3 Feelings** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******Jang 1996 (M): **no genetic variance** — CE model (c²=.39, e²=.61). The only facet in the entire NEO-PI-R with no genetic component in the best-fitting model. **However**: ACE model also fit the data (χ²=0.34 vs. CE χ²=1.65, both ns); CE was selected by AIC parsimony. Jang 1998 (L): raw AE — **the "no genetics" finding did not replicate** in the larger cross-cultural sample. Residual: AE model. Pilia 2006 (XL): no specific anomaly flagged for O3.

        **Developmental trajectory******

        Declines. Terracciano 2005 (L): −1.43 T/decade (steep decline). Schwaba 2022 (M): declines. Brandt 2023 (L): decreased. Schwaba 2022 notes O3 decline is potentially anomalous — no prior study had found this at the time, and related measures of emotional complexity tend to increase with age. Terracciano 2005 reports significant gender interaction: women decline more on O3.

        **Structural placement******

        DeYoung 2007: loads on Openness aspect. Clean single loading.

        **Genetic correlations******

        Nikolašević 2021 (S): rG=.03 with APM — zero genetic correlation with intelligence.

        **Cross-taxonomy******

        Content (emotional awareness/responsivity) overlaps with Neuroticism and Agreeableness more than with aesthetic/imaginative Openness content. HEXACO Emotionality Sentimentality is a partial correspondent.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Content ambiguity.** O3 taps emotional awareness and sensitivity — closer conceptually to N-domain affect or A-domain empathy than to the perceptual/aesthetic content of O2 or the imaginative content of O1. The Jang 1996 (M) "no genetic variance" finding, though not replicated, was interpreted as possibly reflecting that O3 measures something environmentally shaped (emotion socialization) rather than an endogenous exploration trait.

**O4 Actions** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******

        Jang 1996 (M): h²=.41 (additive). Jang 1998 (L): raw AE, residual AE. Pilia 2006 (XL): no age heterogeneity; mild environmental sex heterogeneity but not affecting h².

        **Developmental trajectory****** **Declines consistently**, especially in later adulthood. Terracciano 2005 (L): steep convex decline (accelerating with age). Schwaba 2022 (M): flat (age 30–70). Brandt 2023 (L): decreased; **highest rank-order stability** of any O facet, increasing with age. Soto & John 2012 (M): Adventurousness (≈O4) declines substantially and replicably (b₁=−0.220*, G₁₀=−0.116*) — strongest O decline signal. Soto et al. 2011 (M): part of the decline pattern. Ashton & Lee 2016 (M): HEXACO Unconventionality (partial correspondent) declines ~0.3 SD. Löckenhoff 2009 (M): no retirement response.

        **Structural placement******DeYoung 2007: loads on Openness aspect (.54) — moderate loading. Soto & John 2017: **excluded from BFI-2** as too peripheral to the O/I domain. Briley & Tucker-Drob 2012 (M): Adventurousness (≈O4) loaded primarily on Agreeableness (negative), not Openness — factor loading of .23 on latent O; only 46% global genetic variance. Authors flag this as likely sample-specific (high-achieving adolescents).

        **Genetic correlations******

        Nikolašević 2021 (S): rG=.01 with APM — zero. In stepwise regression, enters as a negative suppressor (β=−0.15).

        **Cross-taxonomy******

        Ludeke 2019 (L): not separately mapped (aspect-level only). The crosswalk notes repeatedly flag O4 as structurally peripheral.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Peripheral to the domain.** Excluded from BFI-2. Weakest loading in DeYoung 2007. Loaded on the wrong domain entirely in one sample (Briley & Tucker-Drob 2012, though sample-specific). Its content (willingness to try new activities, variety-seeking) is more behavioral than perceptual/cognitive, which may explain the structural ambiguity. Developmental decline is consistent but could partly reflect physical energy/health decline in older adults (similar concern to E4 Activity items).

**O5 Ideas** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******

        Jang 1996 (M): d²=.52 (nonadditive). Jang 1998 (L): raw AE, residual h²=.33 (.55 of reliable). Pilia 2006 (XL): no age heterogeneity. Note: O5 and O2 shared nonadditive etiological profile in Jang 1996, but nonadditive effects did not replicate in Jang 1998's larger sample.

        **Developmental trajectory****** **Stable across most of the lifespan**, with some study-specific nuance. Terracciano 2005 (L): essentially flat. Schwaba 2022 (M): flat. Brandt 2023 (L): decreased (but "waved" rank-order stability pattern — lower in midlife, higher in old age). Soto & John 2012 (M): Intellectualism (≈O5) flat. Soto et al. 2011 (M): U-shaped — down in early adolescence, up through adulthood; males higher by ~2–3 T-score points in emerging adulthood. Brandes 2020 (L): ICID-S Intellect flat ages 9–13 while Openness declined — **one of the most replicated facet-level findings** in child developmental literature. Seifert 2023 (XL): intelligence-proxy items track cognitive aging curves — fluid intelligence items rise then decline; crystallized items rise then plateau. Haehner 2025 (L): Intellect aspect shows substantial intercept changes and cumulative change (M_cum=3.25), whereas Openness aspect shows near-zero (M_cum=0.22).

        **Structural placement******DeYoung 2007: **primary marker of Intellect aspect**. Only NEO facet loading clearly on Intellect. Hansen 2025 (M): BFI-2 Intellectual Curiosity β=.07 with musical sensibility when O2 is in model — near-zero, confirming the Intellect/Openness aspect distinction. DeYoung 2015 CB5T: Intellect maps to conscious cognitive exploration (working memory, fluid intelligence).

        **Genetic correlations******Nikolašević 2021 (S): rG=.38 with APM — **highest of any facet in the entire NEO-PI-R**, survives FDR.

        **Cross-taxonomy******

        Ludeke 2019 (L): Intellect aspect poorly captured by HEXACO (R²=.42, β=.27 predicting HEXACO O). This could mean Intellect contains unique Big Five content or is internally heterogeneous. Ashton & Lee 2016: HEXACO Inquisitiveness is the nearest correspondent — rises ~0.3 SD from mid-20s to 70.

        **Dissociation flags******O5 is the **anchor** of the Intellect aspect and behaves distinctively from all other O facets on the intelligence genetic correlation dimension. Its developmental stability dissociates it from the declining O facets (O1, O3, O4). The main anomaly: Intellect is poorly captured by any alternative taxonomy (Ludeke 2019, L), raising the question of whether it's a unique construct or an internally heterogeneous one.

**O6 Values** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******Jang 1996 (M): h²=.44 (additive). Jang 1998 (L): **cross-culturally unstable** — Canada h²=.61 (highest raw h² of any facet in either sample), Germany h²=.38. Residual h²=.35 (.58 of reliable), stably AE. Pilia 2006 (XL): no age heterogeneity reported.

        **Developmental trajectory****** **CONFLICTING — the most contradictory evidence of any O facet.** Terracciano 2005 (L): **steepest decline** of any O facet (−1.93 T/decade, linear). Schwaba 2022 (M): **increases** (~+0.30 SD) — the only O facet that increases significantly. Brandt 2023 (L): **increases** — reversal of Terracciano. Ashton & Lee 2016 (M): HEXACO Unconventionality (partial correspondent) declines ~0.3 SD. Period effects are the likely explanation for the Terracciano vs. Brandt discrepancy (Terracciano 1989–2004; Brandt 2001–2012). The evidence is too conflicted to assign a direction.

        **Structural placement******DeYoung 2007: weakest loading on Openness (.44). **Excluded from BFI-2.** Variable aspect assignment across papers — sometimes Intellect, sometimes Openness.

        **Genetic correlations******

        Nikolašević 2021 (S): rG=.18 with APM — modest positive, consistent with Intellect-adjacent placement. Jang 1998 (L): cross-cultural instability in raw h² but stable residual. The instability is in the relationship between Values and the O factor, not in Values itself.

        **Cross-taxonomy******

        Content is about open-mindedness, tolerance, willingness to re-examine values — conceptually distinct from both aesthetic/perceptual Openness and intellectual curiosity. Closer to a social-attitude facet.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Multiple flags.** (1) Developmental trajectory is flatly contradictory across studies. (2) Weakest structural loading. (3) Excluded from BFI-2. (4) Cross-culturally unstable heritability. (5) Content (tolerance/open-mindedness) is conceptually distinct from the rest of the domain — more social-attitudinal than cognitive or perceptual. O6 is a strong candidate for domain-boundary status or removal.

**Step 2: Three Independent Clusterings**

**Clustering A — Developmental Trajectories Only**

Evidence base: Terracciano 2005 (L), Schwaba 2022 (M), Brandt 2023 (L), Soto & John 2012 (M), Soto et al. 2011 (M), Roberts 2006 (XL, domain-level only), Ringwald 2024 (L, BFI-2 facets), Brandes 2020 (L, childhood ICID-S), Seifert 2023 (XL, item-level), Ashton & Lee 2016 (M, HEXACO), Haehner 2025 (L, BFAS aspects).

**Stable group: {O2 Aesthetics, O5 Ideas}**

Both are consistently stable or flat across adulthood in the best-powered studies (Terracciano L, Schwaba M, Soto & John M). When change appears, it is mild or study-specific. In Seifert 2023 (XL), aesthetic items increase and intelligence-proxy items track cognitive curves — both are "preserved" content types. In childhood (Brandes 2020, L), the analogous ICID Intellect facet was flat while ICID Openness declined. In Haehner 2025 (L), Intellect aspect showed substantial developmental dynamics while Openness aspect was near-inert, but the Openness aspect there has measurement problems (lowest reliability).

Confidence: **Strong signal** for {O2, O5} as a developmentally coherent pair. Replicated across 4+ studies, multiple instruments, and the lifespan from childhood to old age.

**Declining group: {O1 Fantasy, O3 Feelings, O4 Actions}**

All three show consistent decline signals. O4 Actions has the most dramatic decline (especially late-life; Terracciano L, Soto & John M). O1 Fantasy's decline is replicated at the item level across three independent panels in Seifert 2023 (XL). O3 Feelings declines in both Terracciano (L) and Schwaba (M), though Schwaba notes this as potentially anomalous. In childhood, the ICID Openness facet (blending O1/O3/O4 content) declined while Intellect did not (Brandes 2020, L).

Within this group, timing and steepness vary. O4's decline is the most accelerating and may be partly confounded with physical vigor. O3's decline is steepest in the HLM data. O1's decline is the most consistently replicated at the item level.

Confidence: **Moderate signal.** The three facets share direction of change but not timing or magnitude. O4 may be declining for different reasons (behavioral/physical) than O1 and O3 (imaginative/affective).

**Unclassifiable: O6 Values**

Developmental evidence is flatly contradictory. Terracciano 2005 (L): steepest decline. Schwaba 2022 (M) and Brandt 2023 (L): increases. This is likely a period effect rather than a genuine developmental trajectory. No clustering is supportable.

**Clustering B — Structural Mapping Only**

Evidence base: DeYoung 2007, Soto & John 2017, Ludeke 2019 (L), Hansen 2025 (M), Briley & Tucker-Drob 2012 (M).

**Openness aspect: {O1 Fantasy, O2 Aesthetics, O3 Feelings}**

DeYoung 2007: O1 loads .64 on Openness (with .45 secondary on Intellect). O2 loads cleanly. O3 loads cleanly. Soto & John 2017: Aesthetic Sensitivity (≈O2) and Creative Imagination (≈O1, partially) are retained. Hansen 2025 (M): O2 Aesthetic Sensitivity carries essentially the entire domain association with musical sensibility (an aesthetic/affective criterion), confirming it as the Openness aspect anchor.

O1's dual loading (.64/.45) is the weakest placement in this group — it has meaningful secondary affinity with Intellect.

Confidence: **Moderate signal.** O2 is a clean anchor. O3 loads cleanly but with content ambiguity (emotional sensitivity). O1 dual-loads.

**Intellect aspect: {O5 Ideas}**

DeYoung 2007: O5 is the only NEO facet loading clearly on Intellect. Ludeke 2019 (L): Intellect aspect poorly captured by HEXACO (R²=.42) — suggesting either unique content or internal heterogeneity. DeYoung 2015 CB5T: Intellect maps to conscious cognitive exploration, predicts working memory but not implicit learning.

Confidence: **Strong signal** that O5 is the Intellect anchor. But O5 stands essentially alone as a clean Intellect marker in the NEO system.

**Peripheral / unclassifiable: {O4 Actions, O6 Values}**

O4 Actions: loads .54 on Openness (moderate), excluded from BFI-2, loaded on wrong domain in one sample. O6 Values: loads .44 on Openness (weakest), excluded from BFI-2, variable aspect assignment. Both were judged too peripheral to retain in the BFI-2 three-facet structure.

Confidence: **Strong signal** that O4 and O6 are not core members of either aspect.

**Clustering C — Genetic Evidence Only**

Evidence base: Jang 1996 (M), Jang 1998 (L), Pilia 2006 (XL), Nikolašević 2021 (S), Hansen 2025 (M), Briley & Tucker-Drob 2012 (M), Schwaba 2025 GWAS (XL, domain-level only).

**Intelligence-correlated group: {O5 Ideas, O1 Fantasy, O6 Values}**

Nikolašević 2021 (S): O5 rG=.38, O1 rG=.23, O6 rG=.18 with fluid intelligence. All survive FDR or reach significance. Their aspect-mates from the Openness side show zero: O2 rG=−.05, O3 rG=.03, O4 rG=.01.

This is a clean split, but from a single S-quality study. The grouping is notable because O1 Fantasy lands here despite its structural placement on the Openness aspect.

Confidence: **Weak signal.** Single study, small twin sample, wide CIs. The pattern is intriguing but not independently trustworthy.

**Non-intelligence-correlated group: {O2 Aesthetics, O3 Feelings, O4 Actions}**

All three have near-zero genetic correlations with fluid intelligence. O2 has the highest heritability (both raw and residual) and the strongest external genetic correlation with an aesthetic criterion (musical sensibility rA=.69; Hansen 2025, M).

**Etiological type pattern (Jang 1996): {O2, O5} nonadditive vs. {O1, O4, O6} additive vs. {O3} no genetics**This was the sharpest genetic clustering signal for O. However, Jang 1998's (L) larger cross-cultural sample found **no nonadditive effects for any facet**, making the additive/nonadditive distinction unreliable. The O3 "no genetics" finding also did not replicate.

**Overall heritability profile (Pilia 2006, XL): No within-O differentiation.**Pilia explicitly notes that O facets are the most internally consistent domain — no age heterogeneity, no strong sex heterogeneity. This is the highest-quality evidence and it says: **O facets are genetically uniform at the resolution available.**

**Net assessment: Genetic evidence insufficient to independently support within-domain clustering for O.** The intelligence genetic correlation pattern from Nikolašević (S) is the only differentiating signal, and it is underpowered. Pilia (XL) says the domain is genetically homogeneous. Jang 1996's etiological-type clustering was not replicated.

**Step 3: Convergence Assessment**

**Convergence summary**

**No facet pair achieves three-stream convergence.** The genetic stream provides no independent within-domain differentiation (Pilia XL says uniform; Nikolašević S is too weak to count as independent confirmation).

**Two-stream convergence** (developmental + structural):

  - {O1, O3}: both decline, both load on Openness aspect. But note that O1 dual-loads toward Intellect.
  - {O1, O4}: both decline, both load on Openness aspect (O4 weakly).
  - {O3, O4}: both decline, both load on Openness.

These three pairs form a declining-Openness subgroup {O1, O3, O4}, converging on two streams.

**One-stream convergence only:**- {O2, O5}: converge developmentally (both stable) but are assigned to opposite aspects structurally. Genetically undifferentiated from the rest. This is a **cross-aspect developmental convergence** — one of the cleanest signals in the data, but it contradicts the structural mapping.
  - {O2, O3}: structural convergence only (both Openness aspect). Developmentally dissociated.
  - {O5, O6}: weak structural convergence only (both variably Intellect). O6's developmental data is contradictory.

**Proposed within-domain groupings (evidence-driven)**

Given the convergence assessment, the evidence supports a different grouping than the published two-aspect structure:

**Group 1 — Stable/preserved: {O2 Aesthetics, O5 Ideas}** Supported by: developmental convergence (strong signal, multiple studies, multiple age ranges). These two facets are stable across the lifespan while their domain-mates decline. Contradicted by: structural mapping, which places them in opposite aspects. Genetic evidence: silent (no differentiation).

**I need to flag my own pattern-matching here.** The {O2, O5} pairing does not correspond to either the Openness or Intellect aspect. It also does not correspond to any published intermediate structure I'm aware of. The grouping is driven purely by the developmental convergence — both are "cognitively/perceptually preserved" content. Whether this reflects a meaningful latent structure or simply that stable-content facets happen to share a trajectory is an open question.

**Group 2 — Declining: {O1 Fantasy, O3 Feelings, O4 Actions}** Supported by: developmental convergence (moderate signal) and partial structural convergence (all load on Openness aspect, though O1 dual-loads and O4 loads weakly). Contradicted by: nothing directly, but internal heterogeneity is real. O4's decline is behavioral/physical; O3's decline is affective; O1's decline is imaginative. They may decline for different reasons.

**Unclassifiable: O6 Values** Developmental evidence contradictory (strong decline vs. increase across studies). Structurally peripheral (weakest loading, excluded from BFI-2). Cross-culturally unstable heritability. Cannot be confidently assigned to any group.

**Step 4: Misbehaving Facets**

**O1 Fantasy — split allegiances**

O1 is the facet with the most cross-stream disagreement about its placement. Structurally it loads primarily on Openness (.64) — that's its home. But its intelligence genetic correlation (rG=.23; Nikolašević S) aligns with Intellect facets. And developmentally it declines like the other Openness-aspect facets (O3, O4), not like the stable O5 Ideas. O1 sits at the Openness/Intellect boundary in every evidence stream but doesn't consistently lean the same direction across streams.

The dual structural loading (.64/.45) is the highest cross-aspect loading of any O facet and may reflect genuine dual membership rather than measurement noise. DeYoung 2015 CB5T describes Openness as involving "perceptual/aesthetic exploration" and "implicit learning, pattern detection, associational learning." Fantasy/imagination content plausibly involves both perceptual exploration (Openness) and idea generation (Intellect), which would explain the dual loading.

**O4 Actions — domain-peripheral**

O4 is structurally the weakest O facet. Excluded from BFI-2. Loaded on the wrong domain in one sample (Briley & Tucker-Drob 2012, M — acknowledged as likely sample-specific). Its content (willingness to try new activities, variety-seeking) is more behavioral than cognitive or perceptual, making it conceptually distinct from the rest of O.

Its developmental decline is consistent but may partly reflect the same confound as E4 Activity — items about behavioral variety/adventurousness may increasingly measure physical capacity rather than personality in older adults. The crosswalk notes flag this repeatedly.

O4 does not show stronger affinity with any other domain. It is peripheral to O but has nowhere else to go. **Flag for Conversation 6:** Does O4 cluster with any non-O facets cross-domain, or is it a genuinely domain-specific peripheral facet?

**O6 Values — candidate for domain removal**

O6 has the most dissociation flags of any O facet:

  - Contradictory developmental evidence (cannot be assigned a direction)
  - Weakest structural loading in the domain (.44)
  - Excluded from BFI-2
  - Cross-culturally unstable heritability (Jang 1998, L)
  - Content (tolerance, open-mindedness, willingness to re-examine values) is social-attitudinal, not cognitive or perceptual

O6 measures something qualitatively different from the rest of the O domain. Its content is closer to a social-attitude dimension than to the perceptual/cognitive exploration that characterizes both the Openness and Intellect aspects. The cross-cultural heritability instability (Canada h²=.61, Germany h²=.38; Jang 1998, L) further suggests that the relationship between Values and the O factor is culturally variable.

**Flag for Conversation 6:** Does O6 show affinity with A-domain facets (e.g., tolerance/open-mindedness overlapping with Agreeableness)? Or with a political-attitude dimension that doesn't sit cleanly in any Big Five domain?

**O3 Feelings — content ambiguity**

O3's emotional-awareness content is closer to N (emotional reactivity) or A (empathy) than to the perceptual/aesthetic content of the Openness aspect. The Jang 1996 (M) finding of no genetic variance — unique in the entire NEO-PI-R — was interpreted as possibly reflecting environmental shaping (emotion socialization) rather than endogenous exploration. The finding didn't replicate in Jang 1998 (L), weakening this interpretation, but the content concern remains.

O3 declines developmentally like O1 and O4 (its structural groupmates), which is the evidence keeping it in the Openness aspect. But its content places it at the O/N/A boundary.

**Flag for Conversation 6:** Does O3 show genetic correlations with N or A facets?

**Step 5: Domain Coherence Assessment**

**Openness is a moderately coherent domain with a genuine internal fault line.**

The Intellect/Openness aspect distinction is the best-supported within-domain split in the O corpus, but it manifests differently across evidence streams:

**Structurally:** The split is clear. O5 Ideas is the clean Intellect marker; O2 Aesthetics is the clean Openness marker. The aspect factor correlation (.51 in DeYoung 2007) is the **lowest of any domain**, meaning these are the most separable pair of aspects. External criteria cleanly differentiate them (musical sensibility: O2 β=.44, O1 β=.07; intelligence: O5 rG=.38, O2 rG=−.05). Ludeke 2019 (L) confirms the split in HEXACO space (Openness aspect β=.63, Intellect β=.27 predicting HEXACO O).

**Developmentally:** The split does **not** follow the structural boundary. The developmental fault line is {O2, O5 stable} vs. {O1, O3, O4 declining}. O2 (Openness aspect) clusters with O5 (Intellect aspect) on trajectory. O1 (Openness aspect) clusters with the declining group, not with O2. The developmental evidence crosscuts the structural aspects.

**Genetically:** No within-domain differentiation at the best available resolution (Pilia 2006, XL). The intelligence genetic correlation pattern (Nikolašević 2021, S) weakly supports an {O5, O1, O6} vs. {O2, O3, O4} split, which partially matches the Intellect/Openness structure but places O1 on the Intellect side. This is from a single underpowered study.

**The bottom line:** Openness has a real structural split (Intellect vs. Openness), supported by factor analysis, external criteria, and cross-taxonomy mapping. But the developmental and genetic evidence do not independently recover this split. Development says the fault line is "stable/preserved vs. declining," which crosscuts the structural aspects. Genetics says the domain is uniform. The structural split is real — but it may reflect phenotypic organization (how people respond to items) more than distinct biological or developmental processes.

**Comparison to other domains:**

  - N had one confident pairing ({N1, N3}) with three-stream convergence (structural + genetic + partial developmental). O has nothing comparable.
  - E had one decent pairing ({E1, E2, E6}) with two-stream convergence (developmental + structural). O's {O2, O5} pairing is a cross-aspect developmental convergence that contradicts structure — a different kind of signal.
  - A was the weakest domain. O is better — it has a clear structural split and a clear developmental split, even if they don't align.
  - C had moderate domain coherence but no three-stream convergent pairings. O is similar.

**O is in the middle of the pack for domain coherence.** The structural Intellect/Openness distinction is among the best-documented within-domain splits in the Big Five. But it is supported primarily by one evidence stream (structural mapping + external criteria). The other two streams either contradict it (development) or have nothing to say (genetics).

**Step 6: Evidence Gaps**

**Critical gaps**- **No full within-O genetic correlation matrix.** Pilia 2006 reportedly computed genetic correlations among all 30 NEO facets but published them as a cluster dendrogram (Figure 4) and supplementary Table S2 (clusters with rG > .50), not as a full matrix. The full matrix is described as available online. **If retrievable, this would be the single most valuable addition to the O evidence base** — it would directly test whether O2 and O5 share genetic variance (despite being in different aspects) or whether O5 clusters genetically with O1/O6 (as the intelligence correlation pattern suggests).**- No facet-level GWAS data for O.** Schwaba 2025 (XL) operates at domain level only. The O domain showed a unique within-family discrepancy (pop h²_SNP=19.4% vs. within-family h²_SNP=13.4%, p=.02), potentially indicating cultural transmission effects. If Intellect and Openness aspects differ in susceptibility to cultural transmission, this could produce aspect-level dissociations in heritability. But this cannot be tested without facet-level GWAS.**- No genetic factor analysis of O facets.** Pilia 2006's Table S3 (Procrustes-rotated principal components from genetic correlations, targeted to the American normative structure) would give genetic factor loadings. The text references it in passing. This supplementary table is the most direct test of whether the Intellect/Openness structure has genetic reality.**- O6 Values developmental trajectory is unresolved.** The Terracciano vs. Brandt/Schwaba contradiction cannot be adjudicated without a study that controls for period effects (e.g., multi-cohort longitudinal design covering both the 1989–2004 and 2001–2012 windows).**Moderate gaps**

  - **No O3 Feelings genetic correlations with N or A facets.** O3's content ambiguity (emotional awareness overlapping with N and A) could be resolved by testing whether O3 shares genetic variance with N-domain or A-domain facets. No study in this corpus reports this.**- No within-family heritability at facet level for O.** Schwaba 2025's domain-level finding that O is the only domain with significant pop vs. within-family h²_SNP discrepancy suggests cultural transmission effects. Which O facets drive this? Intellect content (ideas, intellectual engagement) is plausibly more susceptible to cultural transmission than aesthetic sensitivity.**- No multivariate genetic model testing the two-aspect structure.** No study fits a two-factor genetic model within O to test whether Intellect and Openness aspects have distinct genetic factors. Briley & Tucker-Drob 2012 (M) tested common vs. independent pathways models at the domain level (Openness as a single factor) and found the common pathway adequate — but with the caveat that their Adventurousness facet contributed almost nothing, masking potential misfit.**- Limited developmental data for O facets in young adulthood (18–30).** Terracciano 2005 (L) is heavily weighted toward ages 60+, with young-adult estimates extrapolated. Schwaba 2022 (M) starts at age 30. The most dynamic developmental period for O (college-age spike per Roberts 2006, XL) is poorly characterized at the facet level.**- No observer-rated developmental data for O facets.** All O developmental evidence is self-report. Jackson et al. 2009 (M) showed self/observer discrepancies for C facets. Whether similar discrepancies exist for O is unknown.**What I have no evidence for**

  - Genetic correlations between any O facet and any non-O facet (except O-facet × intelligence from Nikolašević 2021, S)
  - O facet responses to life events other than retirement (none responded to retirement per Löckenhoff 2009, M)
  - Whether O facets show differential heritability by sex at the facet level (Pilia 2006 reports domain-level sex effects but individual O facets showed only mild environmental heterogeneity)
  - Whether the Intellect/Openness factor correlation (.51 in DeYoung 2007) varies across age, sex, or culture
  - Item-level genetic architecture for any O facet

**Transparency Notes**

**Pattern-matching flag**

The {O2, O5} developmental grouping (both stable) is the clearest cross-aspect signal in the O data. I am not aware of any published model that groups O2 Aesthetics and O5 Ideas together as a unit separate from the rest of O. The grouping falls naturally out of the developmental evidence but has no structural or genetic support. I want to flag that I may be over-weighting this pattern because it is surprising and clean. The alternative interpretation — that stable-trajectory facets happen to come from opposite aspects by coincidence, and the developmental similarity reflects "cognitive/perceptual preservation" as a general aging phenomenon rather than shared latent structure — is equally consistent with the evidence.

**Where this overlaps with published models**

The Intellect/Openness structural split is well-documented and is the basis of the BFAS aspect structure. My structural clustering (Clustering B) naturally recovers this split. This is expected — the structural evidence mostly comes from the same factor-analytic tradition that produced the aspect model. I have not added anything beyond what DeYoung 2007 and subsequent papers already established.

The developmental clustering (Clustering A) does **not** match any published model I'm aware of. The {O2, O5} stable pair and the {O1, O3, O4} declining group crosscut the aspects. This is the novel finding from the synthesis — or, more precisely, the finding that emerges when developmental evidence is weighted independently of structural evidence.

**The Intellect construct problem**

Multiple evidence sources converge on a concern about Intellect as a construct: it is poorly captured by alternative taxonomies (Ludeke 2019, L: R²=.42 by all HEXACO domains); it has only one clean NEO facet marker (O5 Ideas); and the other facets variably assigned to it (O4, O6) are the most peripheral facets in the entire domain. Intellect may be a real construct that is simply under-represented in the NEO-PI-R item pool, or it may be a heterogeneous catch-all for "intellectual" content that doesn't cohere well. This synthesis cannot resolve the question, but the concern should be noted.

**Appendix: Pilia et al. (2006) Supplementary Tables — Impact on O Domain Findings**

The Pilia et al. (2006) supplementary tables (S2 and S3) were identified in project files after the main synthesis was drafted. These are XL-quality data (N=6,148, extended pedigree design) and represent the single most direct test of genetic structure at the facet level for Openness. This appendix integrates the new data and notes where it changes, confirms, or complicates the main synthesis.

**Table S2: Genetic Correlation Clusters (rG > .50 cutoff)**

**Cluster 15: {O1 Fantasy, O2 Aesthetics, O3 Feelings, O5 Ideas}**

  - Average rG = .751 (min .684, max .810)

This is a striking finding. Four of six O facets cluster together at high genetic correlation — and the cluster **crosscuts the published aspect boundary**. O2 (Openness aspect) and O5 (Intellect aspect) share genetic variance at rG > .68. O1 (Openness aspect) and O5 (Intellect aspect) likewise.

**Absent from the cluster: O4 Actions and O6 Values.**

Neither O4 nor O6 met the rG > .50 threshold with the other four O facets. This is consistent with both facets' peripheral status documented in the main synthesis (weakest structural loadings, both excluded from BFI-2).

**Impact on Clustering C (Genetic Evidence)**The main synthesis concluded "Genetic evidence insufficient to independently support within-domain clustering for O." **This conclusion is now overturned.** The Pilia S2 data provide clear genetic clustering:

  - **Core genetic cluster: {O1, O2, O3, O5}** — high shared genetic variance (avg rG = .751). These four facets are genetically cohesive.
  - **Peripheral: {O4, O6}** — do not meet the rG > .50 threshold with the core cluster.

This is a **two-group genetic partition** — but it is emphatically NOT the Intellect/Openness aspect split. The genetic core includes facets from BOTH aspects (O1, O2, O3 from Openness; O5 from Intellect). The excluded facets are the same two flagged as peripheral across every evidence stream.

**Impact on convergence assessment**

The pairwise convergence table needs revision. Key updates:

        **Facet pair****** **Developmental****** **Structural****** **Genetic (revised)****** **Streams converging (revised)******

        O2–O5

        **Converge** (both stable)

        Dissociate (opposite aspects)

        **Converge** (rG > .68, same cluster)

        **2 of 3** ↑ from 1

        O1–O5

        Dissociate (O1 declines, O5 stable)

        Partially converge (O1 dual-loads)

        **Converge** (rG > .68, same cluster)

        **1–2 of 3** ↑ from 1

        O1–O2

        Dissociate

        Converge (both Openness)

        **Converge** (same cluster)

        **2 of 3** ↑ from 1

        Converge (both decline)

        Converge (both Openness)

        **1 of 3** ↑ from 0

The {O1, O3} pair now achieves **three-stream convergence** — the first in the O domain. Both decline developmentally, both load on the Openness aspect structurally, and both are in the same high-rG genetic cluster. This is a strong signal.

The {O2, O5} pair now achieves **two-stream convergence** (developmental + genetic), with the structural stream dissenting. This strengthens the cross-aspect developmental convergence flagged in the main synthesis — it's not just a trajectory coincidence. O2 and O5 share substantial genetic variance despite sitting in different aspects.

**Impact on proposed groupings**

The main synthesis proposed:

  - Group 1 (Stable): {O2, O5} — developmental convergence only
  - Group 2 (Declining): {O1, O3, O4} — developmental + partial structural
  - Unclassifiable: O6

The Pilia data suggest a revised picture:

  - **Genetically cohesive core: {O1, O2, O3, O5}** — these four facets share substantial genetic variance and form the biological backbone of the O domain. They split developmentally (O2/O5 stable, O1/O3 declining), but the genetic correlation data indicate the same genes influence all four. The developmental divergence is likely environmentally mediated — consistent with Briley & Tucker-Drob 2014's (XL) finding that environmental contributions to personality stability increase through adulthood.
  - **Genetically peripheral: {O4, O6}** — do not share high genetic variance with the core. Both are structurally peripheral. O4 declines; O6 is developmentally contradictory. These are candidates for domain-boundary or removal status at the genetic level as well as the structural and developmental levels.

**I need to flag a pattern-matching concern here.** The {O1, O2, O3, O5} core vs. {O4, O6} peripheral split maps cleanly onto the "retained in BFI-2 vs. excluded from BFI-2" decision. Soto & John (2017) dropped O4 Actions and O6 Values from the BFI-2 as too peripheral. The Pilia genetic data independently confirm that these are the two facets with the weakest genetic integration into the domain. This convergence between a psychometric decision (which facets are central enough to retain?) and genetic architecture (which facets share genetic variance?) is notable, though it could partly reflect that peripheral structural loading and low genetic integration are both consequences of the same underlying cause (thin content overlap with the domain core).

**Table S3: Genetic Factor Structure (Procrustes-Rotated PCs)**These are genetic factor loadings for all 30 NEO-PI-R facets, derived from the genetic correlation matrix and rotated to target the American normative (phenotypic) five-factor structure. The O genetic factor congruence coefficient with the phenotypic target is .87 — adequate but the **lowest of any domain** (N = .94, E = .89, A = .96, C = .97).

**O facet loadings on the genetic O factor** **Facet****** **O loading****** **Largest cross-loading****** **Cross-loading domain******

        O1 Fantasy

        **.78******

        .44 (E)

        Extraversion

        O2 Aesthetics

        .42 (E)

        Neuroticism (inverse)

**Interpretation**

**1. All six O facets load on the genetic O factor.** The loadings range from .51 (O4) to .81 (O3). This means the phenotypic domain structure is genetically real — there IS a common genetic factor underlying Openness, and all six facets participate in it.

**2. O4 is the weakest genetic O marker** (.51), consistent with its peripheral status across every evidence stream. O6 loads .70 — genetically more integrated than its phenotypic loading (.44 in DeYoung 2007) would suggest. The genetic structure treats O6 as more central to the domain than the phenotypic structure does.

**3. No genetic Intellect/Openness split is visible.** The genetic factor structure has ONE O factor, not two. O5 Ideas (.80) and O2 Aesthetics (.70) both load strongly on the same genetic factor. O1 Fantasy (.78) loads as strongly as O5. There is no evidence of a second genetic factor separating Intellect from Openness content.

This is the most important finding in the appendix for the aspect-level question: **the Intellect/Openness distinction does not have a separate genetic basis at the resolution available from Pilia (2006)**. The two aspects share a single genetic factor. The aspect-level differentiation is phenotypic — it reflects how genetic and environmental influences combine at the observed level, not distinct genetic pathways.

**4. The dominant cross-loading is with Extraversion.** O1 (.44), O2 (.42), O3 (.41), O4 (.31) all cross-load on the genetic E factor. O5 cross-loads less (.25). This is a systematic E-affinity for the Openness-aspect facets that is weaker for the Intellect anchor. This pattern is consistent with the metatrait structure (Plasticity = E + O shared variance), though I note this observation rather than building on it per the clean-room rule.

**5. O6 Values cross-loads on inverse Neuroticism** (−.28), not on E. This is genetically distinct from the other O facets, all of which cross-load on E. O6's genetic cross-domain affinity is with emotional stability, not extraversion. This is a unique genetic signature for O6, adding another dimension to its "misfit" profile.

**6. O3 Feelings has the highest genetic O loading (.81).** This is ironic given that O3 was the facet flagged for content ambiguity (emotional awareness overlapping N/A) and the Jang 1996 (M) "no genetic variance" finding. The Pilia data (XL) say the opposite: O3 is the most genetically representative O facet. The Jang 1996 finding was almost certainly a small-sample artifact.

**Impact on domain coherence assessment**

The main synthesis rated O as "moderately coherent" with the caveat that the structural split (Intellect/Openness) was the main evidence for internal structure, and development/genetics didn't support it. The Pilia data revise this:

**O is MORE genetically coherent than the main synthesis suggested** — but its coherence is as a single domain, not as two aspects. The genetic factor structure shows one strong O factor. Four of six facets cluster at rG > .68. The factor congruence coefficient (.87) is the lowest of any domain but still in the "adequate" range.

**The Intellect/Openness aspect split is a phenotypic phenomenon, not a genetic one.** At the genetic level, there is one O factor. DeYoung 2015's CB5T description of double dissociation (Intellect → working memory; Openness → implicit learning) may reflect environmental differentiation of a shared genetic substrate, not distinct genetic pathways.

**The real genetic fault line in O is core vs. periphery: {O1, O2, O3, O5} vs. {O4, O6}.** This is not an aspect split. It is a "central to the domain vs. marginal to the domain" split. Both peripheral facets have content that extends beyond the cognitive/perceptual/aesthetic core — O4 toward behavioral variety-seeking, O6 toward social attitudes.

**Cross-Domain Findings from the Pilia Tables (flagged for Conversation 6)**

Several clusters and loadings in the Pilia supplementary data are relevant to other domains already synthesized:

**Cluster 9: {C4 Achievement Striving, E4 Activity} — rG = .775.** This confirms the E4 → C genetic affinity flagged in the E and C syntheses. At the genetic level, E4 is more correlated with a C facet than with most E facets.

**Cluster 18: {E1, E3, E6, N1, N2, N3, N4, N6} — avg rG = .618 (min .233, max .893).** This is a massive cross-domain cluster joining three E facets with five N facets. The rG range (.233–.893) is enormous, suggesting this cluster is held together by some very high pairwise correlations and stretched by some weak ones. This needs decomposition in Conversation 6. The polarity is presumably inverse (high E ↔ low N), but the table doesn't indicate sign — worth checking in the dendrogram or via the loading patterns in Table S3.

**Table S3 cross-loading patterns:**

  - N5 Impulsiveness loads .45 on N, .36 on O, −.41 on C — genetically it sits at the N/O/C intersection, consistent with its "misfit" status.
  - A6 Tender-Mindedness loads .73 on A but .53 on O — the strongest cross-domain genetic loading of any A facet. This is relevant to the O3 Feelings content question (emotional sensitivity shared between O and A).
  - N2 Angry Hostility loads .79 on N but −.35 on A — confirming the N/A genetic entanglement flagged in both the N and A syntheses.

These cross-domain patterns are flagged here but not developed. They belong in Conversation 6.

**Openness to Experience Domain Profile (v2): O1–O6**

**Evidence base:** Soto & John (2017), Jang et al. (2002), Yamagata et al. (2006), Mitrović et al. (2024), Lewis & Bates (2014), Zakrisson et al. (2025), Bleidorn et al. (2009), Haehner et al. (2025), Jackson et al. (2009).

**Step 1: Facet Profile Table**

**O1 Fantasy** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******

        Jang 2002 (L): MZ r = .41/.47 (Canada/Germany), DZ r = .23/.18. Implied h² ≈ .36/.58. Moderate-to-high, somewhat unstable across samples.

        **Developmental trajectory******

        Bleidorn 2009 (M, phenotypic): declined significantly (slope = −1.78*, ages 18–59). Change was entirely environmental (a² = 0, c² = 0, e² = 20).

        **Structural placement******

        Soto & John 2017 (XL): BFI-2 Creative Imagination (partial O1 correspondent) correlates only .38 with NEO Fantasy — notably weak. Authors explain this reflects construct redefinition (creativity/originality vs. idle daydreaming). Creative Imagination has no BFAS aspect assignment (Table 1 shows "—"). DeYoung 2007 placed O1 on Openness aspect (.79 loading, with .45 Intellect secondary).

        **Genetic structure******

        Jang 2002 (L): German genetic factor 1 (Openness-like): O1 loads .41. Factor 2 (Intellect-like): O1 loads .17. Sits with the Openness factor, consistent with DeYoung's assignment. Yamagata 2006 (XL): O1 loads on genetic O factor across all three countries — part of the genetic core.

        **Dissociation flags******

        BFI-2's Creative Imagination (the nearest BFI-2 counterpart) is deliberately orphaned from both BFAS aspects — Soto & John judged it as falling between Openness and Intellect. Change is entirely environmentally driven (Bleidorn 2009, S for biometric claims).

**O2 Aesthetics** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******

        Jang 2002 (L): MZ r = .51/.47 (Canada/Germany), DZ r = .11/.21. Implied h² ≈ .80/.52. Canada estimate is very high; cross-sample instability is notable. Bleidorn 2009 (S for biometric): change has genetic component (a² = 14) — unusual within the O domain where most facet change is environmental.

        **Developmental trajectory******Bleidorn 2009 (M, phenotypic): **stable** (no significant slope, ages 18–59). One of three O facets that did not change.

        **Structural placement******

        Soto & John 2017 (XL): BFI-2 Aesthetic Sensitivity r = .76 with BFAS Openness, .74 with NEO Aesthetics — clean alignment. Haehner 2025 (L): Aesthetic Sensitivity is the strongest Openness aspect marker. Hansen 2025 (from extraction notes in Soto & John): β = .44 with musical sensibility.

        **Genetic structure******

        Jang 2002 (L): German genetic factor 1 (Openness-like): O2 loads .54 — the highest loading on this factor. Canadian: .68. O2 is the anchor of the genetic Openness factor in both samples. Yamagata 2006 (XL): O2 is part of the genetic O core across all three countries. Lewis & Bates 2014 (M): HEXACO Aesthetic Appreciation has the most facet-specific genetic variance (a1 = .42) of any O facet.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Etiological outlier for change.** O2's developmental change has a genetic component (a² = 14; Bleidorn 2009, S for biometric), while O1's and O3's change is entirely environmental. This is within-Openness-aspect etiological heterogeneity. Cross-sample heritability instability (.80 Canada vs. .52 Germany) adds another note of caution.

**O3 Feelings** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******

        Jang 2002 (L): MZ r = .37/.31 (Canada/Germany), DZ r = .14/.21. Implied h² ≈ .46/.20. Substantial cross-sample instability. German estimate is low.

        **Developmental trajectory******Bleidorn 2009 (M, phenotypic): **stable** (no significant slope, ages 18–59). Change entirely environmental.

        **Structural placement******

        Soto & John 2017 (XL): Not directly mapped by the BFI-2 (no direct counterpart). DeYoung 2007: loads on Openness aspect. Yamagata 2006 (XL): O3 has mild E and N satellite loadings in the genetic factor structure — consistent with its content overlapping emotional awareness/reactivity.

        **Genetic structure******

        Jang 2002 (L): German genetic factor 1 (Openness-like): O3 loads .50. Canadian: .49. Loads cleanly on the Openness genetic factor in both samples. Yamagata 2006 (XL): part of the genetic O core, but with mild cross-domain genetic loadings on E and N.

        **Dissociation flags******

        Content (emotional awareness/responsivity) overlaps with N and A more than with aesthetic or intellectual content. The E/N genetic cross-loadings in Yamagata 2006 support the content concern — O3 is not genetically pure O.

**O4 Actions** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******

        Jang 2002 (L): MZ r = .32/.39 (Canada/Germany), DZ r = .19/.14. Implied h² ≈ .26/.50. Cross-sample instability.

        **Developmental trajectory******

        Bleidorn 2009 (M, phenotypic): declined significantly (slope = −1.37*, ages 18–59). Change entirely environmental (a² = 0).

        **Structural placement******Soto & John 2017 (XL): **excluded from BFI-2** as too peripheral to the O/I domain. Zakrisson 2025 (L): O has worst CFA fit of any BFI-2 domain.

        **Genetic structure******Jang 2002 (L): German genetic factor 2 (Intellect-like): O4 loads .70 — **the highest loading on this factor** and 100% on factor 2 (zero on factor 1). Canadian: O4 loads .44 on factor 2. **This is the cleanest single-factor facet in the O domain.** Yamagata 2006 (XL): O4 genetic loading weakens dramatically across cultures: .65 (Canada) → .43 (Germany) → .37 (Japan). In Japan, O4 fails to load ≥ .40 on ANY genetic factor. O4 is the weakest genetic member of the O domain.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Multiple convergent flags.** (1) Excluded from BFI-2. (2) Most cross-culturally unstable genetic loading of any O facet (Yamagata XL). (3) Loads 100% on the Intellect genetic factor in Germany but has no clear genetic home in Japan. (4) Content (behavioral variety-seeking) is more behavioral than cognitive or perceptual. Despite being assigned to the Openness aspect phenotypically (DeYoung 2007), O4 loads exclusively on the Intellect genetic factor in the clearest data (Jang 2002, German sample). This is a structural-genetic mismatch.

**O5 Ideas** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******

        Jang 2002 (L): MZ r = .49/.44 (Canada/Germany), DZ r = .16/.16. Implied h² ≈ .66/.56. Relatively consistent across samples. High facet-specific genetic variance: 44–55% of genetic variance is specific, not shared with domain common factors.

        **Developmental trajectory******Bleidorn 2009 (M, phenotypic): **stable** (no significant slope, ages 18–59).

        **Structural placement******

        Soto & John 2017 (XL): BFI-2 Intellectual Curiosity r = .72 with NEO Ideas, .62 with BFAS Intellect — clean alignment. Haehner 2025 (L): Intellect correlates positively with self-esteem (.29) and weakly with political progressivism (.10); Openness shows zero SE and stronger political link (.23). Aspects are sharply divergent in external correlates.

        **Genetic structure******Jang 2002 (L): German: loads .36 on genetic factor 2 (Intellect-like) and .29 on factor 1 (Openness-like) — **cross-loads**, with much of its variance specific (44–55%). Canadian: .52 on factor 1, .30 on factor 2 — loads MORE on the Openness factor in Canada. O5 is genetically semi-independent of its domain. Yamagata 2006 (XL): O5 is part of the genetic O core across all three countries.

        **Dissociation flags****** **High genetic independence.** 44–55% facet-specific genetic variance means much of what makes O5 heritable is not shared with the rest of the O domain. Cross-loads on both genetic factors in Jang 2002 rather than sitting cleanly on Intellect. The Intellect/Openness external correlate divergence (Haehner 2025, L) is the strongest evidence these aspects are distinct constructs, but O5 doesn't sit cleanly on its own genetic factor.

**O6 Values** **Dimension****** **Evidence****** **Heritability******Jang 2002 (L): MZ r = .35/.34 (Canada/Germany), DZ r = .30/.31. Implied h² ≈ .10/.06. **Near-zero heritability by Falconer's formula** — DZ correlations are almost as high as MZ. Elevated DZ correlations suggest possible shared environment effects. 51–59% facet-specific genetic variance — the most genetically independent O facet.

        **Developmental trajectory******Bleidorn 2009 (M, phenotypic): **stable** (no-growth model fit best, ages 18–59). Jackson 2009 (M): Conventionality (≈ reverse O6) increased only in late adulthood — a unique late-onset trajectory.

        **Structural placement******Soto & John 2017 (XL): **excluded from BFI-2** as too peripheral. DeYoung 2007: weakest loading (.44). Variable aspect assignment across papers. Haehner 2025 (L): Openness-Intellect within-domain aspect correlation = .29 — the lowest of any domain, meaning the two aspects are nearly independent. O6's variable assignment between these nearly independent aspects is a further flag.

        **Genetic structure******Jang 2002 (L): German: loads .39 on factor 2 (Intellect-like), .17 on factor 1 (Openness-like). Canadian: .42 on factor 2, .10 on factor 1. Weak loadings on both factors in both samples. 51–59% of genetic variance is facet-specific — **the most genetically independent facet in the O domain.** Yamagata 2006 (XL): moderate genetic O loading across countries.

        **Dissociation flags****** **Multiple convergent flags.** (1) Near-zero implied heritability (Jang 2002). (2) Highest facet-specific genetic variance in the domain. (3) Excluded from BFI-2. (4) Weakest structural loading. (5) Content (tolerance, open-mindedness) is social-attitudinal. (6) Elevated DZ correlations suggest shared environment, atypical for personality. O6 is the strongest candidate for domain-boundary status or removal.

**Step 2: Three Independent Clusterings**

**Clustering A — Developmental Trajectories Only**

Evidence base: Bleidorn 2009 (M for phenotypic, S for biometric). This is the only study in this corpus with facet-level developmental data for all six O facets. Jackson 2009 (M) adds peripheral evidence via Conventionality (≈ reverse O6).

**Declining: {O1 Fantasy, O4 Actions}**

Both show significant negative slopes (O1: −1.78*; O4: −1.37*) over ages 18–59. Both decline are entirely environmentally driven (a² = 0 for both). These are the two "active" facets in O — content about imagining (O1) and doing (O4).

**Stable: {O2 Aesthetics, O3 Feelings, O5 Ideas, O6 Values}**

All four show no significant mean-level change. O6 was fit by a no-growth model (not even individual differences in slope). O2, O3, and O5 had nonsignificant slopes.

However, the stable group is etiologically heterogeneous: O2's (non-significant) change has a genetic component (a² = 14), while the others' change is environmental or nil. This is within-group heterogeneity in mechanism despite trajectory similarity.

Confidence: **Moderate signal.** Based on a single study with modest N (344 individuals, 126 MZ pairs). The declining/stable split is clear in direction but the study covers only ages 18–59 — late-life trajectories are unknown from this corpus.

**Note:** The developmental evidence in this corpus is thin — one study. Direction is usable; precise magnitudes and biometric decompositions are not independently trustworthy.

**Clustering B — Structural Mapping Only**

Evidence base: Soto & John 2017 (XL), Zakrisson 2025 (L), Haehner 2025 (L).

**Openness aspect: {O2 Aesthetics, O3 Feelings, and partially O1 Fantasy}**

Soto & John 2017 (XL): Aesthetic Sensitivity maps cleanly to BFAS Openness (r = .76). DeYoung 2007: O2 loads on Openness, O3 loads on Openness, O1 loads primarily on Openness (.79) with Intellect secondary (.45).

O1's partial status: The BFI-2's Creative Imagination (nearest O1 correspondent) is deliberately orphaned from both aspects (no BFAS assignment in Table 1). The weak convergence between Creative Imagination and NEO Fantasy (.38) means the BFI-2 doesn't directly test O1's aspect placement — it redefined the construct.

Haehner 2025 (L): Openness aspect correlates near-zero with self-esteem (−.02) and moderately with political progressivism (.23). External correlate profile is distinct from Intellect.

**Intellect aspect: {O5 Ideas, and partially O4 Actions and O6 Values}**

Soto & John 2017 (XL): Intellectual Curiosity maps cleanly to BFAS Intellect (r = .62) and NEO Ideas (.72). O4 and O6 are excluded from BFI-2 entirely — too peripheral.

Haehner 2025 (L): Intellect correlates positively with self-esteem (.29) and weakly with political progressivism (.10). Sharply divergent from Openness on SE (.29 vs. −.02).

O4 and O6's structural status: Both load weakly and were excluded from BFI-2. Structurally, they are variably assigned to Intellect but sit at the domain periphery.

**Domain-level structural observations:**- Openness-Intellect within-domain aspect correlation = .29 (Haehner 2025, L) — the **lowest of any domain**. These are the most separable aspects.
  - O has the worst CFA fit of any domain in BFI-2 (Zakrisson 2025, L).
  - Only metric (not scalar) invariance cross-culturally for O (Zakrisson 2025, L).
  - Gender: women higher Aesthetic Sensitivity, men higher Intellectual Curiosity and Creative Imagination (Soto & John 2017, XL).

Confidence: **Strong signal** for the Openness/Intellect structural split. The aspect correlation of .29 and divergent external correlates make this the most documented within-domain distinction. But O4 and O6 are structurally peripheral to both aspects.

**Clustering C — Genetic Evidence Only**

Evidence base: Jang 2002 (L), Yamagata 2006 (XL), Lewis & Bates 2014 (M), Bleidorn 2009 (S for biometric). Mitrović 2024 (S) contributes a null: no COMT associations with any O facet.

**Two genetic factors within O (Jang 2002, L):**

This is the key finding. For O, a single genetic factor was rejected; two were needed. The German sample gives the cleaner picture:

  - Genetic factor 1 ("Openness-like"): O2 Aesthetics (.54), O3 Feelings (.50), O1 Fantasy (.41)
  - Genetic factor 2 ("Intellect-like"): O4 Actions (.70), O5 Ideas (.36), O6 Values (.39)

This maps directly onto DeYoung's (2007) aspect structure, published five years later. In Germany, {O1, O2, O3} and {O4, O5, O6} are genetically separable.

**But the Canadian sample is messier:** O5 Ideas cross-loads .52 on factor 1 (Openness-like), blurring the boundary. And across both samples, O5 and O6 have very high facet-specific genetic variance (44–59%), meaning much of their heritability is independent of both common genetic factors.

**Cross-cultural genetic stability (Yamagata 2006, XL):**

O1, O2, O5 are the stable genetic core across Canada, Germany, and Japan. O6 is moderate. O3 has mild E/N cross-loadings. O4 Actions is the problem child — its genetic loading drops from .65 (Canada) to .43 (Germany) to .37 (Japan), failing to load ≥ .40 on any factor in Japan.

**HEXACO genetic structure (Lewis & Bates 2014, M):**

All four HEXACO O facets load on a common genetic factor (.41–.59). Common pathway model adequate (with modifications). This is a single-factor genetic structure — no within-O genetic split detected. But HEXACO-60's 2–3 items per facet likely lack the precision to detect sub-domain structure.

**Proposed genetic clustering:**

  - Core Openness genetic factor: {O1, O2, O3} — consistent across Jang 2002 samples
  - Intellect genetic factor: {O4} loads cleanly in Germany; {O5, O6} load weakly with high specific variance
  - O5 Ideas is genetically semi-independent — cross-loads or loads more on Openness factor depending on sample
  - O6 Values is the most genetically independent O facet (51–59% specific)
  - O4 Actions is the most cross-culturally unstable (Yamagata XL)

Confidence: **Moderate signal.** The two-factor genetic structure within O is the cleanest of any domain in Jang 2002, but the Canadian/German discrepancy and the high facet-specific variance for O5 and O6 introduce uncertainty. The Openness genetic factor {O1, O2, O3} is robust. The Intellect genetic factor is fragmented — O4 loads cleanly but is cross-culturally unstable; O5 cross-loads; O6 is mostly independent.

**Step 3: Convergence Assessment**

**Pairwise convergence table** **Facet pair****** **Developmental****** **Structural****** **Genetic****** **Streams converging******

        O1–O4

        **Converge** (both decline)

        Dissociate (different aspects)

        Dissociate (different genetic factors, Jang 2002 L)

        **1 of 3** (developmental only)

        O2–O3

        **Converge** (both stable)

        **Converge** (both Openness aspect)

        **Converge** (both load on genetic factor 1, Jang 2002 L)

        **3 of 3******

        O1–O2

        Dissociate (O1 declines, O2 stable)

        Converge (both Openness aspect)

        Converge (both genetic factor 1)

        **2 of 3** (structural + genetic)

        O1–O3

        Dissociate (O1 declines, O3 stable)

        Converge (both Openness aspect)

        Converge (both genetic factor 1)

        **2 of 3** (structural + genetic)

        O2–O5

        Converge (both stable)

        Dissociate (opposite aspects)

        Dissociate (different genetic factors in Germany; O5 cross-loads in Canada)

        **1 of 3** (developmental only)

        O5–O6

        Converge (both stable)

        Weakly converge (both variably Intellect)

        Partially converge (both on genetic factor 2, but high specific variance)

        **1–2 of 3******

        O4–O5

        Dissociate (O4 declines, O5 stable)

        Partially converge (both assigned to Intellect by some)

        Partially converge (both on genetic factor 2 in Germany)

        **1 of 3** (weak genetic)

        O4–O6
